Chapter 451: Faceless 15 Minsk Street, in the dank, cold kitchen. Klein rummaged for his newly purchased large iron pot, poured fresh water into it, and carefully scrubbed it several times. Then he dropped some matches into it and snapped his fingers. The scarlet flames soared, and under his control, they quickly burned the remaining water droplets dry, without harming the surface of the pot. This time, there were no ingredients such as pure water in the potion recipe, so Klein was even more careful than on the previous two occasions. He wanted to ensure that every measurement was correct to prevent any problems. Although divination could tell him whether the concoction would succeed, or at least whether it would endanger his life, a failed result could still render the potion useless. Extracting the Beyonder characteristics from a failed concoction would be relatively difficult, just like removing a Rampager's mental corruption. He would need the corresponding techniques, methods, or rituals, and Klein wouldn't be able to gather a second round of ingredients within a short period of time either. After finishing his preparations, Klein took a deep breath as he looked at the neatly arranged boxes. Finally, he recalled the potion recipe: "Faceless: Main ingredients: Mutated pituitary gland of a Thousand-faced Hunter, characteristic of a Human-skinned Shadow. Supplementary ingredients: 80 ml of a Thousand-faced Hunter's blood, 5 drops of black Jimsonweed juice, 10 grams of Dragon Tooth Grass powder, 3 strands of hair from a Deep-sea Naga." He first picked up the cardboard box which came from Vampire Emlyn White. He opened the box and took out the glass bottle that contained 100 milliliters of a Thousand-faced Hunter's blood. Watching the graduated markings, Klein unscrewed the lid and poured the viscous liquid into the pot with a steady hand. The liquid changed its color depending on the variation in the lighting. As it was a supplementary ingredient, he didn't insist on absolute precision and didn't use laboratory apparatus to separate and titrate the liquid. The Thousand-faced Hunter's blood was like thin honey that slowly filled the bottom of the pot. When Klein judged that he had poured enough, he stopped tilting it and allowed the liquid to flow back. There's still about 20 milliliters left; my spiritual intuition is quite accurate... Klein withdrew his gaze from the glass bottle and tightened the lid. These 20 milliliters of Thousand-faced Hunter's blood could still be used to make mystical items, Beyonder weapons, mystical scrolls, or for drawing symbols or magic labels for certain rituals. It was still very precious. After putting the glass bottle back into the cotton-filled box, Klein continued down the list and added the black Jimsonweed juice and the Dragon Tooth Grass powder. He saw that the liquid in the iron pot started to bubble. Without pausing, he picked up three strands of dark blue hair that resembled tiny snakes with his black-gloved right hand, and he carefully laid them on the surface of the liquid. Sizzle! A faint mist rose from the iron pot, and the color of the liquid turned a deep blue. It already has such a strange effect even without adding the main ingredients... As expected of a Sequence 6 potion... Klein reached out and grabbed the silver tin box which Vampire Emlyn White had thrown in as well. With a snapping sound, Klein opened the lid of the box, revealing a peach-like item inside. He didn't directly touch the Thousand-faced Hunter's mutated pituitary gland. Instead, he placed the tin box above the pot and overturned it. A yellow-brown object with the grooves and ridges of a brain fell in, crashing into the dark blue liquid. At this moment, no liquid droplets splashed up, and the pituitary gland that was constantly changing its shape silently melted. Gray and yellowish-brown quickly merged with the dark blue, and the bubbles suddenly swelled. At this point, Klein was a little nervous, but he controlled himself. He picked up the last box and overturned the huge diamond-like Human-skinned Shadow characteristic into the pot. All of a sudden, the mist converged, and even the glow of the gas lamp showed signs of being attracted and devoured as the room turned darker. When everything returned to normal, Klein finally saw the final potion's appearance. It was blackish-green all over, and from time to time it produced a bubble the size of an eye. As though alive, it also made a burping sound every few seconds. When the bubble reached the surface, it would immediately burst, and during this process, it would reflect light in a myriad of colors. The colors combined to form a picture of different faces, and the facial features seemed to be a random combination. Klein picked up the iron pot with one hand and poured the liquid inside into a glass bottle that he had prepared beforehand. Due to the potion's properties, not one bit was left in the pot. Using divination to confirm that the degree of harm was acceptable, which meant that the potion had been successfully concocted, Klein took the Faceless potion bottle and walked steadily back to the second floor, entering the room with the curtains closed. After locking the door, he sat on the edge of the bed and, with the help of Cogitation, calmed his slightly agitated and anxious mood. After sitting upright for more than ten seconds, Klein twisted the cap off the bottle, raised his right hand, and swiftly poured the entire potion into his mouth. A slightly tingling sensation spread through his mouth and esophagus, and finally, it fermented into a numbing sensation that made him lose his senses. Klein's psyche seemed to be extracted from his body as he felt like a bystander and saw his mouth, nose, ears, and eyes melting--his entire face was melting! In just two or three seconds, his face and head had turned into what seemed like scorched white wax. His body was similarly abnormal; it was as if his bones and skin were being melted by blood. No, I can't allow such a situation to continue developing! Klein knew that if he didn't keep it under control, he could be on the verge of losing control at any moment. As a "spectator," he tried his best to pull his thoughts back to his body, trying to visualize the layers of stacked spherical lights and maintain his state of Cogitation. After a brief but repeated attempt, he finally felt his body again. He tried his best to control every tiny part of his body to keep himself from crossing the limit. As the melting and dissolving assaulted him again and again, Klein lost sense of how long he had held on, but when it finally came to an end, he regained the feeling that his body belonged to him. At that moment, he knew that he had finally broken through the threshold and was now a Sequence 6. He was now a Faceless! Klein didn't sweat, but he was mentally exhausted. He barely got to his feet and walked to the full-length mirror, trying to observe what he looked like. Under the gas lamp's illumination, he took two sudden steps back and caused the floor to reverberate. The scene in the mirror was extremely shocking! Klein saw that his face and exposed skin were covered in dense, pale granules. It would make any normal creature who saw them instinctively recoil in disgust. The more timid ones might even lose their minds. Despite knowing the acting method, and having completely digested the Magician potion, my advancement to Sequence 6 was still rather difficult. I was only two to three steps away from losing control. I wonder how much of a risk those Beyonders who relied on the passage of time and barely qualified to consume the potion faced. The probability of failure definitely isn't low... It's no wonder Sequence 7 is relatively common among the members of the Churches, and they shoulder the responsibility of captain or bishop, while the number of people at Sequence 6 begins to plummet... Klein closed his eyes and sat back in his chair. With the help of Cogitation, he was able to restrain his overflowing spirituality and recover from his exhaustion. After about ten minutes, the disgusting granules on his body had completely faded and merged into his flesh. Phew. Klein exhaled, walked back to the full-length mirror, and examined his heavily bearded appearance. Suddenly, his facial hair began to squirm. His flesh and skin began to undergo a bizarre change like half-melted wax. Within a second or two, Klein had reverted to his original appearance: black hair, brown eyes, ordinary facial features, a clean-shaven face with well-defined contours, and a somewhat thin body. He quietly gazed at his past self and stretched out his right hand to press it against his face. With a gentle touch, Klein's face changed again. His nose bridge was high, his lips were extremely thin, and there was a tinge of elegance in his handsome face. There was an unconcealable pride in his appearance. It was none other than Vampire Emlyn White. A little too short... He laughed at himself. At this moment, the bones and ligaments in his body began to produce cracking and snapping sounds as his entire body suddenly shot upward. He now looked identical to Emlyn White, at least outwardly. I've noticed that I can accurately recall the outward appearance and temperament of every person I know, and I can grasp their unique scent... Klein's pores squirmed, and his body changed accordingly. The smell of his body became different as a result of this. He looked into the full-length mirror again, letting his hairline slowly recede, and changing the color of his eyes into a deep gray. Without a sound, Dunn Smith seemed to come alive again, and he was staring at the team member who always came to him to report on unusual matters. Letting out a breath, Klein returned to his own form, but the corners of his mouth remained curled upwards. He thought for a moment, took a few steps back, and looked at the magazine Ladies Aesthetics, which he had bought to study fashionable beauties, committing the appearance of the actress on the cover to memory. Then, he went back to the full-length mirror and wiped his face with his right hand. When he looked in the mirror again, he saw a young lady with black hair that went past her shoulders and delicate facial features. It really does work... Klein looked down at his chest, but he didn't see any protrusions. Trying hard to control his body, he managed to create an A-cup bust by moving his fat and some of his flesh. However, there was nothing he could do about his lower body. That is to say, it is just a superficial change in appearance, not a fundamental transformation... Also, I can only grow taller or shorter by 10 centimeters at the moment. Going beyond this range would be difficult... Also, there's a limit to the enlargement and shrinkage of the head. For example, I can't mimic Father Utravsky's half-giant head at all... Also, I can only observe the appearance and temperament of the target in an instant, without knowing anything related to him. It's easy for me to be exposed when facing a Spectator like Miss Justice... Heh heh, in the Clown stage, the Clown restrains the Spectator, but in the Faceless stage, it is instead somewhat countered by the Spectator. Interesting... Upon having this thought, Klein stopped changing and returned to the appearance of Sherlock Moriarty. Divination, fighting, as well as a Magician's Beyonder powers, have all been enhanced. As for how much they've improved, it can only be confirmed through practice. I should find some time to head to the Quelaag Club tomorrow... Klein took one last look and went downstairs to clear up the mess. After tidying everything away, he quickly washed up and crawled into bed. Lying there, looking out the window at the crimson moonlight, he gradually calmed down. After a few minutes of this, Klein slowly closed his eyes and smiled to himself. Good night, Faceless. Chapter 452: Benson's Decision Townshend Street, in between Empress Borough and West Borough. Xio Derecha was standing in a dark, secluded alley. Even without looking up, she could see the countless magnificent palaces and tall Gothic towers in the distance. It was the highest district in the whole of Backlund, and it was also where the Loen royal family resided. It shared the same or even slightly higher status as the White Maple Palace of Intis and the Aurmir Palace of the Feysac Empire in the Southern and Northern Continent and in the entire world, but its name was neither romantic nor ancient. It was called the Sodela Palace, and in ancient Feysac, it meant "balance." Xio looked away from the famous "Bell of Order" and turned her gaze to the other side of the alley. In the shadows where the street lamps didn't shine, a figure slowly walked out. The figure wore a golden mask that revealed the lower half of his face. He was none other than the mysterious person who had previously sold the Sheriff formula to Xio and had occasionally entrusted her with some tasks. When Xio and Fors discussed him in private, they both suspected that the person was from MI9. "Any progress this week?" the man wearing the gold mask asked as usual. Xio shook her head. "No, I don't think anyone was keeping tabs on Capim before the incident." She paused for a moment, then she reluctantly asked, "Do you still want to continue investigating this matter?" The masked man was silent for a moment. "There's no need, but if you hear anything related in the future, then contact me right away. "Today, I'll give you a new mission." "What mission?" Xio was fully in bounty-hunter mode, ready to assess the risks. The masked man laughed. "This is a very simple mission, and it's one that you've been dreaming of doing. "Seek out and purchase the Sheriff and Interrogator main ingredients in all your circles, especially a special ingredient that can be directly concocted into a potion. If someone responds, we will pay for it." "The purchased ingredients will belong to me?" Xio blurted out. This was her greatest concern. "No, do you believe that such a simple mission would have such a high reward? Of course, if you're able to draw out the person we're looking for, it's not impossible to negotiate and let you obtain the main ingredients," the masked man said with a chuckle. "But I don't know the main ingredients of the Interrogator potion..." Xio said hesitantly. "I'll tell you later. This is also our advance payment. Even if we don't find the target, you'll know the main ingredients for a Sequence 7 potion formula, information worth more than 600 pounds. I think you're well aware of our generosity," the masked man said in a tempting tone. Generous indeed... Who's their target, and why are they willing to pay such a high price to find them? Well, if he were from MI9, using this mission to retrieve the Beyonder ingredients of the Arbiter pathway on the market should also be one of his goals. Spending money wouldn't be a concern... As a rather experienced bounty hunter, Xio instinctively thought of a few things. After some deliberation, she nodded and said, "I'll take this commission." "Very good." The masked man's tone turned relaxed as he surveyed his surroundings and said, "The Interrogator potion's main ingredients are the horn of a Flash-patterned Black Snake and dust of a Lake Spirit." After saying that, he slowly retreated and melded into the shadows, disappearing into the corner of the alley. "He really told me the main ingredients of the Interrogator potion..." Xio was momentarily stunned. Only at that point did she clearly realize how much importance the masked man's faction placed on this mission. I wonder who their target is. The search seems focused on some special ingredient that can be used to directly concoct a Sheriff or Interrogator potion... With this in mind, Xio suddenly froze. She remembered one thing. When she advanced to Sheriff, she had used a special ingredient she had bought through Fors, one that could be used to concoct the corresponding potion! This... Is that their target? Xio instinctively decided to keep silent on this matter, to never tell the masked man anything. She rubbed her round, childish cheeks and walked towards the street outside, ready to take a public carriage back to Cherwood Borough. At that moment, she saw a brown carriage drive past. Her eyes were instantly drawn to the coat of arms on the carriage. The coat of arms consisted of a single flower and two rings. There was nothing special about it, but Xio stared at it in a daze as though her gaze had frozen. Only after the carriage had traveled far away did she withdraw her gaze. Her mood immediately reached rock bottom. It didn't improve, even when she returned to the house she rented with Fors. Seeing that her friend was in a bad mood, Fors poured two glasses of red wine and brought them to her. "What happened?" She sat across from her and pushed one of the glasses toward Xio. Xio looked down at the red wine. After a full two minutes of silence, she said in a slightly hoarse voice, "On the way back, I met someone I used to know." "Who?" Fors asked, cooperatively. "Viscount Stratford," Xio answered without elaborating. Fors thought for a moment and then she said, "This Viscount seems to be the captain of the royal guards?" As a best-selling novelist, she would occasionally receive invitations to afternoon teas and banquets from noblemen who enjoyed literature. Given her professional habit of gathering material as a writer, she naturally learned about prominent nobles. It was at such a party that she got to know Viscount Glaint. "Yes, he used to be my father's deputy," Xio said with some difficulty. "Your father?" Fors knew that Xio came from a fallen aristocratic family and had some secrets. However, she didn't know the specifics. Xio picked up the glass of red wine and gulped it down, coughing a few times after she choked. After calming herself down, she said, "My family was once part of the aristocracy. In its heyday, it even served as an earl palatine." "Earl palatine? What position is that?" Fors asked, half curious, half trying to soothe her friend's mood. "It means serving as the royal family's spokesperson, as one of the nobles closest to the throne," Xio said, recalling the past with a proud light on her face. "From that time onward, our family held the fief of a true earl, but it was no longer as illustrious by my father's time. However, we still had the trust of the former king, Might-wielder William VI. My father was the leader of the Household Guards Division and the captain of the royal guards." Her tone gradually turned melancholic as she found it difficult to hide her pain. "But seven years ago, he was accused of participating in a revolt, and he was eventually executed and deprived of his aristocratic title and fiefdom. "Because of this, my family fell from grace, and many of my family members even died for no reason. In order to live, we changed our surnames and left East Tucker County... "I don't believe that my father would engage in mutiny. He was so loyal to the royal family, even more devoted to it than he was to the Goddess! In order to... In any case, I left my mother and my younger brother and came to Backlund, looking for a chance to improve myself and hoping to restore my family's glory and my father's reputation." Xio was vague about some of the things in the middle of her account, but Fors didn't mind. She sighed and said, "It'll be very, very difficult." But she immediately smiled. "But I will support you!" And behind me is the mysterious Tarot Club! she added in her mind. Nighttime in Tingen City, 2 Daffodil Street. Melissa, who was doing math problems, turned her head to the door and looked at Benson who had just taken off his half top hat. "Where did you go? Aren't the results announced tomorrow?" "They're already available tonight, and I happened to know two of the city employees who are in charge of the unified exam," Benson said with a smirk. In early December, he had registered for the Civil Servant Unified Examination, selected a relatively uncompetitive position, and, in the process, established friendships with several civil servants and a number of examination candidates through his eloquence and communication skills. And you can take advantage of the New Year's holiday to transfer to the technical school in Backlund to prepare for the entrance exam at the Backlund University of Technology and Industry in June." Just a month ago, the Backlund Polytechnic Institute of Technology and Industry had been reorganized and officially upgraded to a university for industry and technology. Klein walked out and hired a carriage to head for Prince Edessak's Red Rose Manor. It was the place where Talim had gone to a few days before his death. Paper Figurine Substitutes and Damage Transfer didn't experience much change, but because of the growth of his Spirituality, the number of times they could be used had increased. I don't want to get into trouble... Klein laughed casually. "Alright!" Chapter 453: Stairs Outside a luxurious room filled with all kinds of antiques. Klein stood at the door and asked the sixth maid he had met today. The young maid was wearing a black-and-white maid's uniform that was typical of the era. She was pretty, in the bloom of youth, and had naturally curly brown hair. She had a hint of playfulness on her face. "Who does Mr. Talim meet when he visits?" Klein repeated the question numbly. The maid replied almost without a pause, "Mr. Talim usually asks to meet His Highness, or to accompany him in riding, or to discuss matters. If His Highness happens to be absent, he will meet with that lady. They are very good friends. He always obtains permission from the butler." Talim is good friends with the commoner lady who Prince Edessak likes? Meeting in private from time to time? It was likely that he was persuading her to leave this place, so as not to affect the reputation of the Prince... Klein nodded thoughtfully. With his rich "experience" from the information age, he somehow came up with an extremely contrived romance story. The maid wasn't afraid of the detective. She smiled, shook her head, and said, "At times like these, we're all asked to leave the room." This... Even more than ever, Klein couldn't resist making associations, even to the point of changing the colors of Prince Edessak's hat and helmet. Without waiting for him to ask any further questions, the maid chuckled and said, "Detective Moriarty, if you want to find out exactly what the lady and Mr. Talim were talking about, you can ask her directly." You even know to call me Detective Moriarty." The maid looked around, kept smiling, and said, "It's because I've been serving the lady during my rotations. She's always wanted to meet you, Detective Moriarty. After all, she's close friends with Mr. Talim and cares a great deal about his death. "Unfortunately, she always ends up missing you." "Always?" Accompanied by two maids, she slowly disappeared into the distance... One of the ladies had naturally curly brown hair. He wanted to leave Red Rose Manor before the young lady returned from her ride! At four o'clock in the afternoon, before the sky had turned dark, Klein left the manor much earlier than expected and took the carriage sent by the old butler back to the city. I hope that the Machinery Hivemind, with my earlier reminder, will attach importance to the royal family's abnormality. Yes, they might regard it with great importance--not because of Prince Edessak's reputation, but for another reason! I hope the royal family continues to prevent the lady from visiting me... After a few days, when the time is right, I'll relinquish this mission, express how powerless I am on this matter, and then use the excuse of going to the south for a holiday, to change my identity and hide for a while! Klein's heart gradually calmed down. From the looks of it, that should be the case. Yes, I should try to explore the area and see if there are any changes. Klein slowly stood up and followed his spiritual perception, walking in a certain direction outside the ancient palace. Beneath his feet was the gray fog that spread out like the sea. It was holy, transparent, and untainted, capable of shocking anyone's heart. It was a significant distance from the staircase of light. The four-step staircase represents my consumption of the Sequence 9, Sequence 8, Sequence 7, and Sequence 6 potions? What exactly is above that layer of fog? Klein cautiously advanced until he reached the staircase of light and stepped onto it. There was nothing odd about the steps, and they were as solid as stone. Klein went up the stairs, climbed up to the fourth level, and then tried to look up at the area above the gray fog. Unfortunately, his vision couldn't penetrate anything. He thought for a moment, then he took two steps forward and jumped up with all his might. However, the moment he left the staircase of light, he lost his momentum and his Spirit Body's flight instinct. He plummeted straight down and landed on the bottom layer of the gray fog. Looks like I need to advance another two or three Sequences. If Sequence 4 really does result in a qualitative change, then I should be able to see after becoming a demigod... Klein looked up and made a judgment. Chapter 454: Who Am I? After looking around, Klein, who had found nothing else, returned to the ancient palace supported by stone pillars. Including the empty positions that I could handle from before, I can still recruit four new members. However, there are no suitable targets at the moment... Klein shook his head and muttered before returning to the real world to prepare a hearty dinner. After slicing the potatoes, boiling the beef, adding onions, sauteing them for a while, and sprinkling in seasonings such as sugar and pepper, Klein poured the prepared hot water into the saucepan, covered it with a lid, and turned the heat down. I have to admit that a Magician's Flame Controlling is a good helper in the kitchen... Ever since I got it, my culinary skills have clearly improved... If it wasn't for the loss of control, the monsters, the searches, and the evil gods, the world would be at peace and perfect if everyone was dedicated to using their abilities without causing public damage... Klein sighed and left the kitchen to enter the living room. As light from the wall lamps spilled into the room, Klein planned to flip through some magazines while waiting for the right moment to add the potatoes, carrots, and the proper amount of salt, but he couldn't help thinking about how to act as a Faceless. Just after waking up this morning, my spirituality had completely stabilized. I discovered that although there aren't any signs of digesting the Faceless potion in me, I do have a certain level of harmony. This is a phenomenon that never happened after consuming the Seer, Clown, and Magician potions... With this in mind, Klein looked up at the oriel window. As it was already dark outside, it had become like a mirror, faithfully reflecting Sherlock Moriarty with his black hair, brown eyes, beard, and gold-rimmed glasses. Klein nodded thoughtfully. Perhaps it's because I've always been acting as Klein Moretti. Yes... In a way, Sherlock Moriarty is just Klein's disguise--not someone else. Although I've received many memory fragments and inherited some of the original Klein's feelings, in essence, I'm still a guest from an alternate world. I am Earth's keyboard warrior, Zhou Mingrui. I've been through so much in the last five months that sometimes I even think I'm Klein Moretti. Amidst the silence, many thoughts flashed through Klein's mind, giving him a lot of insight. However, I'm still Zhou Mingrui who's donning the skin of Klein Moretti... Someone who has never given up the idea of returning... He slowly closed his eyes. When he opened them again, the figure reflected on the oriel window had already changed. It was a young man with dark brown eyes and short black hair. His facial features were unremarkable, and he appeared refined, but he had obvious eye bags with a hint of a double chin. This was Zhou Mingrui from Earth. Long time no see... Klein sighed, raised his hands and rubbed his face. By the time he lowered his arms, he was back as Sherlock Moriarty. After this period of contemplation and adjustment, he inexplicably felt that the gulf between his spirituality and the potion had narrowed significantly, and there were signs of them slowly blending together. No wonder Marionettist Rosago's teacher--yes, it was probably his teacher--would say that you can disguise yourself as anyone, but you are ultimately yourself... This is likely a core principle of a Faceless's acting method. Once one forgets this point, it would be easy to lose oneself amidst the constant changes, eventually turning into a monster. Klein felt enlightened when he recalled what he had previously obtained via spirit channeling. He crossed his right leg and leaned back, quickly coming up with a plan for the future. Search for and conclude the actual acting principles for a Faceless... In the Beyonder circles of Backlund, through the Tarot Club, gather information about mermaids, in preparation to complete the ritual at sea... Directly or indirectly get the Solar High Priest potion formula to help Little Sun advance to Sequence 7, so that he has the clearance to obtain the method for removing a Rampager's mental corruption on a Beyonder characteristic. But I can't fully place my hopes on Little Sun. I should still attempt to look. Klein, whose mood was gradually settling, snapped his fingers, reducing the flame beneath the stove in the kitchen as the fragrance of beef emanated. At that moment, he heard the doorbell ring. "The new year..." The lightning gradually subsided, and the darkness became the ruler of the earth. The exploratory team from the City of Silver finally reached their destination after a journey that was brief in distance yet arduous in experience and numerous battles. On both sides of the street, most of the houses had collapsed. Only a few could barely hold on, but their surfaces were mottled with the dust of time. With the help of an animal hide lantern whose light didn't reach far, Derrick Berg saw the completely collapsed walls and streets that were in such ruin that even weeds didn't grow. On both sides of the street stood half-collapsed houses, with a tiny minority barely standing. However, their surfaces were mottled and filled with signs of aging. The white and blue colors that formed the foundation of the coatings, the pointed structures which were different from the City of Silver, had already turned gray, making it hard for anyone to imagine their original appearance. However, Derrick could figure out the city's past through such observations. It had definitely experienced a long history with a considerable number of inhabitants, developing its own civilization in the dark ages. The humans here took potions, built and repaired buildings, and defended the city wall. About six teams or more had explored the outside and hunted monsters in search of the necessary resources to survive. They would celebrate for any brief periods of calm, offering sacrifices to the gods and yearning for a response. They gave birth to the next generation, allowing hope to continue. However, in the end, they disappeared into the darkness, losing all their voices and leaving behind only ruins. The ruins were like a huge grave, burying a civilization that had struggled to survive, only to perish in desolation. Demon Hunter Colin looked around, his expression somewhat solemn, as though he saw the future of the City of Silver. He pointed ahead and said, "The other places have already been cleared. "The temple is in the center of the city." The exploratory team spread out slightly, but they maintained proper formation and didn't let down their guard. After passing through ruins that had been destroyed for an unknown period of time and passing along streets so quiet that they drove people mad, Derrick finally saw a high, wide man-made platform. On top of the high platform, there was a half-collapsed building. It looked extremely similar to the temples in the City of Silver. It had a dome propped up by pillars, creating an arched entrance. The style of the buildings here was completely different from that of the other buildings. It really is true that they later converted to the Fallen Creator... The thought had barely crossed Derrick's mind when the four lanterns in the team went out at the same time! All of a sudden, the exploratory team was plunged into pure darkness. There was no lightning in the sky, the candles on the ground had gone out, and all human breathing seemed to disappear at once. Derrick's body immediately tensed. He felt as if some monster had stuck out its tongue in the darkness and was trying to lick his scalp, but his spiritual perception told him that nothing real had appeared. At that moment, an immature, helpless, terrified, and dry voice sounded beside his ear. It was a child whispering, "Save me... Save me..." For a moment, Derrick froze, not knowing how to react, but an instant later, a coruscating glimmer of dust appeared before him. The dust exploded one after another, igniting a silvery white light that illuminated the surrounding area. Colin stared at Derrick and said in a deep voice, "What are you thinking about?" Derrick instantly snapped to his senses and clasped his hands in shame and pressed them to his mouth and nose as if in prayer. His body immediately emitted rays of clean, pure light, causing the surrounding darkness to silently disperse. The other team members seized the moment to reignite the candles. Because of the Demon Hunter's timely reaction, no members vanished this time, nor did any new members appear. Colin retracted his gaze from Derrick and looked at the half-collapsed temple on the high platform. He solemnly said, "From now on, we can't afford to be careless. We have to be on full alert." Chapter 455: Help Seeker Within the half-collapsed temple, only a few stone pillars remained intact as they collectively supported half of the main hall. At the very front of the main hall, there was an altar covered in cracks. In the middle of the altar, there was a huge, pitch-black cross. Hanging upside down from the cross was a naked man. Rusty metal spikes protruded conspicuously from his ankles, thighs, and torso beside bright red bloodstains. Derrick knew that this was the statue of the Fallen Creator, but he couldn't help lowering his gaze to size up the statue's face. He saw the face of the statue--the nose, mouth, and ears were all blurry, except for the eyes which were carved with abnormal clarity. The Fallen Creator's eyes were tightly shut, as though he was suffering from guilt and pain. "Look away, don't study the statue of an evil god!" Demon Hunter Colin warned in a low voice. Before today, although the City of Silver had discovered many destroyed cities and written records pointing to evil gods during their explorations, most of the residents had never seen the statues of so-called evil gods. Author's Note: The second volume runs to about 850,000 Chinese characters, making it a very long volume by my standards. I believe only the third and fourth volumes of The Sage Who Transcended Samsara were longer. I have spent every day thinking and writing. Although I have been very happy, I am truly exhausted. I even began exercising this month and have scarcely had time to read any novels. This volume is titled "Faceless," which is intended to convey three meanings. Please note that what follows is the standard answer to the reading-comprehension question. The first is Klein's advancement to Faceless, the simplest and most obvious meaning. The second represents Klein's life in Backlund. He uses false faces, names, and identities to meet people, handle affairs, and become embroiled in disturbances. Yet when he returns home, he is still alone in an empty house. The third refers to those crushed to dust or swept away by the tides of a great era. In history books, they have no names, faces, pasts, or lives; they exist only as simple figures or descriptions. In a sense, are they not also "Faceless"? Who cares what the least conspicuous cannon fodder looked like? Consider the sentence, "When one is hungry, one eats another." How much sorrow, pain, cruelty, bloodshed, and despair are contained in those few simple words? How many living people, stripped of faces and names, are compressed within them? Tens of thousands, hundreds of thousands, millions! Therefore, amid the tides of the era, I tried to depict Old Kohler, Liv, and Freja, restoring the hopes, hardships, and tragedies of these "Faceless" so that they appeared as people rather than numbers. Although this second aspect is a major reason the volume feels diffuse, it is also the hidden main thread. As they passed away, more people like them streamed in. The "Faceless" have always existed. They are the stepping-stones of the age, its pioneers, and indispensable figures in the story as a whole. With this in mind, I abandoned a more sorrowful and shocking ending. After all, this volume is about the faceless. Reversing and echoing "the best of times" and "the worst of times" expressed what I wanted to say with far greater force. The age advances, merciless and implacable, but countless faceless people lie beneath its feet. The title of the third volume will not have so many symbolic meanings, because the main line is very clear and direct. Well, the name of the third volume is "Traveler". Judging from the title, everyone should be able to imagine something, but our focus will remain on the latter aspect. Many threads in Backlund have yet to be gathered: the Snake of Mercury, the underground evil spirit, the royal family's true scheme, 0-17, the Sanguine, and the Psychology Alchemists. This is because Audrey, Fors, Xio, and Emlyn need opportunities to distinguish themselves in Backlund, and because Backlund is the most important stage in the entire novel. Klein will certainly return and remain there for a long time. People have said the second volume felt scattered. In fact, once many of its events were connected, I thought it worked well enough. To me, a diffuse but compelling narrative is not necessarily bad. The real problem lay elsewhere: the story's rhythm remained tense and compressed, without enough room to breathe. In other words, the characters constantly encountered incidents and solved problems. The volume lacked sufficiently long, relaxed transitions, making it somewhat tiring and oppressive to read. Although this produced the special effect of characteristics converging, the structure did create problems. The problem is that the Tarot Club meets once a week, dividing the timeline into one-week segments. This can easily feel repetitive and inflexible, while also requiring certain events to occur each week. Consequently, the pace of Beyonder incidents becomes rather tight. The solution I am considering is to skip some less important Tarot Gatherings and handle the timeline more freely. Of course, the Tarot Club itself is also central to the story, and there will certainly be no shortage of its gatherings. I have not forgotten that this was Emlyn's first meeting, nor the many matters readers mentioned, such as the Churches' reaction and the other bronze whistle. I remember them all, but deliberately held them back until the beginning of the third volume. This was to keep minor matters from diluting or damaging the overall imagery and emotion of the final two sections, so some technical handling was necessary. With the second volume complete, I am delighted to see its characters established one by one and embraced by everyone. Ending matters decisively with 0-17 was not out of the question. On one hand, it showed how formidable the Churches are; they have remained the ruling powers for so long for good reason. On the other hand, that was not the ending itself, merely a continuation that links what came before to what follows and points toward later developments. I will say no more, lest I spoil anything. Finally, in writing novels, I increasingly pursue simplicity and penetration. Whatever can be described accurately with the plainest, simplest words should not be drawn out or smothered in ornament. Emotion should accumulate step by step through calm, objective prose, then, at precisely the right moment, the most exact and simple language should pierce the barrier and strike the softest place in your heart. Although many problems remain, I am still on the journey. Of course, the nature of serialized web fiction means I can achieve this only in the most important chapters, where it is needed most. It is impossible to write every chapter that way. Many things can only be revised when I am older and have time to spare. In general, my vocabulary is extensive but unfamiliar to me; words accumulated little by little are useless if they do not come naturally. When writing habitually, one draws from the list of words most readily available in the mind, so repetition is difficult to avoid. Similarly, in trying to present the clearest, most immediate, and most important images in my mind, I sometimes misuse adjectives or attempt to coin expressions. This still requires exploration and improvement. The above is the technical summary of the second volume. Writing is arduous and torturous, but once the words are written, receiving praise is delightful; I feel exceptionally happy and fulfilled. Recently, everyone has praised me for being full of enthusiasm. Gentlemen, I like to write novels! I like to tell you stories! I like creating vivid, fully realized characters one by one! I like to show you an interesting and novel world! Writing novels is really a joy! Well, it is astonishing that today's highest subscription count on the Qidian platform is 53,000, with every chapter above 41,000. The twenty-four-hour subscription count is 27,000 to 28,000, and the latest chapters have officially surpassed 31,000. This shows that reader retention is high, that most who begin reading continue to follow the story, and that all of you are full of love! The recommendation votes have also remained in the top fifteen, and the monthly tickets in the top five. I am too lazy to manipulate any figures. Therefore, every vote, every reward, and every subscription is filled with love! Gentlemen, the second volume is over. I want to ask for praise and a monthly ticket! I am asking for monthly ticketssssssss! Finally, according to convention, we will take two days off: Monday and Tuesday. We will return at the usual time, 12:30 p.m. on Wednesday. What? You are asking about Sunday? I didn't hear you, I didn't hear you... All right, I am joking. Let me rest for half a day after such a long volume. "Which one of you is going to use 2-111?" All the Machinery Hivemind deacons and captains looked at each other. For a moment, no one responded. The sound of water splashing against the ship's hull seemed to be the only sound left in the world. The sea at night was both noisy and quiet. Klein suddenly awoke and opened his eyes. He saw the wooden ceiling bathed in the crimson moon's veil. His spiritual intuition told him that something was happening outside. Is someone having a rendezvous? He tilted his head to listen, vaguely able to hear some unnatural sounds. He sat up, put on his gloves, and put on his coat. His eyes turned dark as he took out a gold coin, flipped it, and quickly performed a divination. After receiving no revelations of danger, he took out the revolver under his pillow and put it in his pocket. Once suitably prepared, Klein opened the door and walked out of the room, following the sound to the upper deck. At this moment, far out at sea, away from industrial pollution, the crimson moon quietly hung there, mysterious and dreamy. After carefully bypassing a few patrolling crew members, Klein arrived at the area where the disturbance originated. He could smell the faint scent of blood. With the help of the moonlight, he looked over and saw the former adventurer, Cleves, crouching by the side of the ship and arranging something. There were three people hiding in the shadows of the cabin about a dozen meters away from this gentleman. One of them was Cleves's companion, the female bodyguard in the black coat, and the remaining two were the children of their employer, a girl of fourteen or fifteen and a young gentleman no older than ten. The two youngsters wore thick cotton nightgowns and outer coats. It was obvious that they had come out in a hurry. They trembled amidst the chilling night wind, but they still crouched there brimming with energy, looking at Cleves with their bright eyes. Playing hide and seek? Klein joked inwardly. He deliberately made his footsteps louder, causing Cleves and the others to turn their gazes toward him. "Friend, what happened?" Klein adopted the manner of certain bounty hunters he had known in East Borough. But he still maintained Gehrman Sparrow's distinctive cold, incisive demeanor. The reason he had named himself Gehrman was that it was the name of the first hunter in a game he had played in his previous life, and it suited his idea of hunting evil at sea. Klein was in no hurry to inquire about the reason. With the hand that wore Creeping Hunger, he pointed toward the nearby shadows. "Private job? Doing a private job in front of your employer?" Cleves, who was squatting there, glanced at the boy and the girl and said, without changing his tone, "Cecile wasn't careful enough and ended up waking Donna and Denton up. She had no choice but to let them follow." The girl called Donna wrinkled her nose when she heard her name mentioned. She looked up curiously and asked Klein, "Uncle, are you also an adventurer?" Uncle? Even counting my age on Earth, I'm at most ten years older than you! Klein said in amusement, "No, you can't use the word 'also.' Strictly speaking, I'm the only adventurer here; they're just bodyguards right now." He turned to Cleves and said, "Hey, mate, what prey did you discover?" Cleves looked into the faint crimson waters and said, "A murloc." Murloc? That's a Beyonder creature! Even though it's among the lowest level, it's still quite difficult for ordinary people to handle. They would need at least five or six people and four or five spears to have a chance... That's right, the scales on a murloc's body are very hard. Revolvers can inflict only minor damage. They would need rifles... Klein raised his eyebrows and asked, "What do you plan on doing? One to two hours ago, it tried to climb onto the ship to attack the passengers, but the deck was still bustling with activity and there were lots of sailors and crew." Klein took a few steps forward and saw that there were some traces of green corrosion on the side of the ship. He recalled the information he had encountered in Tingen, which agreed with the books. He asked with great interest, "Why does it have to be alone rather than part of a group?" He remembered that murlocs tended to live in groups. "If it's a group, they'll simply destroy the ship's hull and let everyone sink. Moreover, the area around this channel and the surrounding sea have already been cleared of murlocs. The Church of Storms really enjoys hunting them," Cleves solemnly explained. That's because murlocs are probably one of the main ingredients of the Sequence 9 Sailor potion... Klein stroked the revolver in his pocket and asked with a smile, "Are you confident?" Cleves didn't directly answer him and instead opened a paper bag beside him. Inside the bag were some pig organs still stained with blood. This was the source of the blood Klein had smelled. "All murlocs like this kind of food and are unable to resist its allure. Of course, these monsters love human organs the most, so in many sea legends, they recommend preparing some pig or beef organs from the ship's kitchen or canned organs," Cleves said as he sprinkled some granules. "Pepper granules can cause the murlocs to experience a marijuana-like exhilaration and lose some of their sense of balance. This can last for about a minute, and after that, the murlocs will be exhausted after the heightened excitement subsides." He then took out a wooden box from his clothes and smeared the dark green paste over the tip of the triangular blade, dagger, and short knife. "The mint cream that's popular in Pritz Harbor is a unique sweetener for humans, but to murlocs, it's a deadly blood toxin. "In addition, I borrowed two rifles from the sailors. I secured an agreement that this area would remain undisturbed for twenty minutes, and I spent a sizable sum of money. However, as long as I can successfully kill a murloc, then I'll be able to earn ten, twenty, or even thirty times my costs." As expected of an experienced adventurer, he's exceptionally knowledgeable about his prey's weaknesses and habits... Listening to him speak, I feel like they have a chance of successfully hunting the murloc, even if they aren't Beyonders... In the face of traps and firearms, Low-Sequence Beyonders really aren't much stronger than the average person... It's not like there are no Low-Sequence Beyonders who die in gang wars... However, murlocs are creatures that seem to wear full-body armor. It's not easy to kill them. It might be injured, but it could still escape... Cleves didn't react to the praise as he remained calm and silent. As they conversed, the girl Donna and the boy Denton squatted in the shadows and listened with relish. They found all of this to be the most interesting thing in the world. Yes, I also need to brush up on my studies in this area... Klein smiled and said, "So that's how it is. I didn't disturb you, did I?" Chapter 494: A Bite of a Murloc The red moon hung high in the sky as Klein walked over to Donna and Denton and squatted down beside them. Cecile, Cleves's female companion, let out a sigh of relief. She picked up the rifle on the deck, bent down, and quickly moved in another direction. She put about ten meters between herself and the peppered pig organs. "Uncle, is it starting...?" The mischievous, freckled teenage girl, Donna, suddenly felt a little nervous. However, her face was filled with curiosity and anticipation. Klein raised his left index finger to his mouth, motioning for the two youngsters to be quiet. At times like this, he couldn't help but thank Roselle. It was because of the efforts of this senior transmigrator that some of his habitual gestures had become common body language in the Northern Continent, without causing misunderstandings. It was said that in the early part of the Fifth Epoch, this "don't talk" gesture was an insult in Loen. But in certain parts of the Southern Continent, it means "kiss me"... It was a monster bathed in crimson moonlight. Its entire body was covered in dark green scales, and green slime flowed over it. It didn't have much in common with humans. It was like a giant fish that had grown four strong limbs, and there was conspicuous webbing between its digits. The murloc was more than 1.9 meters tall, with round eyes and gills on its cheeks. It resembled a demon from legend, making Donna cover her mouth to prevent herself from screaming. At the same time, she covered the mouth of her brother, Denton, as well. Good thinking... The light in its mostly white eyes gradually faded, as if it had fallen into a dream. It isn't very intelligent... Klein shook his head and made his judgment. Bang! Cleves pulled the trigger, and a bullet flew from the rifle. It instantly struck the murloc's chest, causing its scales to shatter and blood to splatter. "Wa!" The murloc let out a childlike cry, and it threw itself at Cleves, who was hidden in the shadows. It was as fast as a steam train. At this moment, Cecile, who was in another position, opened fire as well. Bang! The bullet from the rifle struck the murloc's ribs, causing many scales to scatter and the tall figure to stagger. The murloc, having eaten the pepper granules, became visibly sluggish. It stopped, unsure of which enemy to attack first. This gave Cleves and Cecile a chance to calmly reload. They took aim again and pulled their triggers. Bang! Bang! Blooms of blood erupted in succession, and the pain restored clarity to the murloc's eyes. It flipped and pounced, dodging the follow-up shots, and closed in on Cleves as though uninjured. Cleves calmly set down the rifle in his hand and picked up the triangular blade that was leaning to his side. Instead of dodging, he leaped forward and rolled to the side of the murloc. The triangular blade in his hand ruthlessly and accurately pierced the patch on his prey's side where the scales had shattered. The murloc spun around abruptly, stirring up a gust of wind. It violently flung away both the triangular blade and Cleves, causing the former adventurer to crash onto the deck. The murloc shook its head, as though overcome by intense discomfort. It didn't attack Cleves and Cecile again, but instead took large strides to the side of the ship in an attempt to jump into the sea. Bang! Cecile's bullet hit it again, raising another bloom of blood, but still failing to stop it. With two steps, the murloc reached a suitable spot. It bent its knees and prepared to jump up. However, its body was too weak to exert its full strength. Its leap clearly fell short, so it could only land back on the deck. Bang! The murloc withstood the shot and tried to flip over the side of the ship. Seeing that it was about to escape, Klein took out his revolver. Just then, a loud bang came from another direction! The murloc's left eye turned into a bloody hole, and one could vaguely see a pale gelatinous substance squirming inside. It wasn't dead yet. It lay on the deck, trying its best to crawl and get up again. After a few seconds, the poison took effect and it twitched and died. Chapter 495: Treasure Legends An extra layer of asbestos had been placed beneath the grill on deck to prevent the coal sparks from being blown out by the wind and scorching the surface of the ship. The east and west sides of the Southern Continent were similar to those of the Northern Continent, while to its south was the Polar Sea. Together, they formed the Five Seas. The Loen Kingdom was backed by the Hornacis mountain range and the inland Midseashire. Its eastern coast was the Sonia Sea, and it stretched south to Desi Bay. It occupied several of the best entrances to the Berserk Sea, but it had no access to the Fog Sea. "Is that so..." As a merchant's daughter who had been educated for many years, she had a vague understanding of what that sum represented. Even though I know it's nearly impossible to find the treasure--otherwise, the various Churches would've done so already with all their powerful Beyonders--I can't help getting excited when discussing such things. Chapter 497: The Bounty Wall After their discussion of the treasure legends that had inspired generations of adventurers to take to the seas ended, the cook had also finished roasting the flesh of the murloc's belly. Klein was very serious when it came to tasting delicacies, so he was in no hurry to eat the fish. Immediately afterward, the meat's delicious juices, the slightly astringent sea salt, and the refreshing sweet-and-sour taste of lemon all burst forth at once, filling his mouth and making it water. If he hadn't accepted the ten pounds, then in the eyes of a veteran adventurer like Cleves, it would appear as though he was dissatisfied with the price and was attempting to get more, as though he might attack them at any moment. Among self-proclaimed adventurers, there was definitely no shortage of greedy madmen! Seeing Gehrman Sparrow pocket the money, Cleves looked away and said flatly, "This is a rule of the sea." If I could get such rewards from every mission of this difficulty, then I would've made a fortune long ago as a private detective... Chapter 498: Persona One participant in the standoff was a brawny man in a blue-and-white striped shirt that marked him as a navy sailor. Despite the near-freezing temperature, his arms were bare. The gold coin kept weaving around in his fingers as though scouting his surroundings. Chapter 499: Recruitment Ding! "Dogsh*t, just two years ago, we missed a good chance of finding the Specter Empire because Captain was on holiday!" Pirates have annual leave? Klein found it somewhat surprising. He could only feel the pirate crew's comic absurdity from the man's description, reminding him of the humorous Somali pirate recruitment advertisements he had seen in his previous life. Seeing that Klein appeared shocked by his words, the black-cloaked man added with a smile, "As an adventurer, are you still chasing after the supernatural powers spoken of in legends? "As long as you join us, you'll have a chance to possess them!" Having said that, he coughed and said, "I forgot to introduce myself." His expression turned solemn, his earlier jocularity gone. "Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina Edwards's subordinate, Fourth Boatswain of the Golden Dream, Blazing Danitz." After reporting his title, real name, and identity, Danitz waited patiently for the look of panic and fear to appear on Klein's face. After a second, he heard the adventurer, who behaved somewhat madly despite his refined, polite appearance, say in a low voice, "Blazing Danitz with a bounty of 3,000 pounds?" Danitz was about to reply when he had the illusion that the man standing in the dark alley had turned into an indescribable ravenous monster, salivating over his soul and flesh. He suddenly clenched his fists; his body was no longer relaxed. He was so tense that he was trembling slightly. His intuition told him that he was facing an abyss filled with madness and bloodlust! In this state, Danitz had no idea how much time had passed before the other party spoke again. "Why are you here?" "I-I'm on vacation..." Blazing Danitz's dignity rebelled at answering the question, but his instincts forced the words out. As soon as he finished speaking, he felt the man retract his gaze, the hunger to gnaw on his flesh and soul disappearing. He stood where he was and watched the young adventurer in a black coat and silk half top hat turn and walk to the other end of the alley. Just before rounding the corner, the adventurer stopped, looked back, and asked, "Where's White Shark?" "H-he lives at 1 Sea Wall Avenue, but he spends most of his time on the second floor of the Flying Fish & Wine. It's no different today," Blazing Danitz answered truthfully. Only when the figure disappeared into the distance did Danitz straighten his back and take off his cloak. "What a terrifying fellow..." He sighed silently. Following that, he collected his thoughts. I have to inform Captain that there's another terrifying person at sea. This is a guy who looks like a gentleman on the outside but has the heart of a lunatic. If one doesn't have the determination and confidence to kill him, it's best not to deal with him. Danitz pulled up the hood of his black cloak and decided to return to the hotel for some sleep. He planned to wait until the telegraph office opened the next morning before relaying the message to his intermediary in the Rorsted Archipelago. As for what would happen to White Shark, he didn't care at all. In the corner of another quiet street, Klein stood in the shadows, looking down at the black glove on his left hand. He found that, although Creeping Hunger had been sealed by Mr. Azik, its inherent thirst for flesh and souls remained and was trying to manifest itself. Under normal circumstances, Klein wasn't worried that the Sealed Artifact would cause any problems, but when he had the urge to kill someone, its influence was enough to release the hunger that might recoil upon him. Earlier, when he heard that Danitz was a famous pirate on the bounty list, his desire to hunt instantly gave rise to killing intent, causing Creeping Hunger to become as lively as a fish in water. Fortunately, Klein always had excellent self-control in this regard. From Danitz's words, he was able to judge that the pirate wasn't steeped in sin, so he easily restrained his impulse. With Creeping Hunger, Gehrman Sparrow's persona will be flawless... Klein paused for a few seconds, took out a gold coin, and performed two divinations. First, he divined whether Blazing Danitz was lying, and second, whether White Shark Hamilton could harm him. The first revelation showed that there was no need for Blazing Danitz to lie, and the second one showed that White Shark Hamilton was unable to cause harm to him. Klein put away the gold coin, pressed down his hat, and while doing so, he swept his palm down and touched his face. He instantly changed his appearance--blond hair, blue eyes, and ordinary facial features! Then, Klein unbuttoned his overcoat and pulled out his shirt so that it was no longer tucked into his trousers. After this simple change in attire, Klein, who didn't carry a cane, appeared sweaty and dry-lipped as he identified the direction and headed back to the Flying Fish & Wine bar! On the way, he met Blazing Danitz again. The man only glanced at him once before withdrawing his gaze and heading for the hotel opposite the bar. After inspecting the bounty wall, Klein calmly reached out with his hand, pushed open the door, and walked in. At this moment, it had been less than ten minutes since he escaped. In the bar, most of the customers had dispersed, but there were still many drunkards gathered here to watch the show. Their eyes swept over the new guest, but they all looked away soon after, and Klein made his way unimpeded to the bar counter. He saw the bartender standing fearfully beside a fat man. The bartender's forehead was swathed in white bandages, his nostrils were stuffed with tissue, and his face was bruised. The fat man was tall and broad. His skin was fair and he looked like a big white shark that had swum ashore. He touched his shiny bald head and said to Elland, who was wearing a dark red jacket and a straight sword, "I heard you know that guy. "There are only three passenger ships that docked today, so there won't be many unfamiliar outsiders. Don't try to lie!" Elland patted the hilt of his sword and smiled casually. "Yes, he is my passenger. "But the problem today clearly stemmed from your people." "So I just want him to come back here, apologize to me, and compensate me for the damage done to the bar," the large, pale fat man said with a frown. Elland laughed and said, "White Shark, I have a proverb from my hometown: 'Don't hate the wild dogs passing by because of the rats in your storehouse.'" "Just Elland, is that your answer?" White Shark Hamilton narrowed his eyes. Elland gripped the wooden handle of the musket, took a step forward, and said in a deep voice, "Yes, that's my answer!" Captain sure has his way of doing things... Klein was slightly surprised at Elland's response. After staring at each other for a few seconds, White Shark Hamilton drew a deep breath. "You've helped me before, so I don't need an apology. However, he must compensate half of my losses, and you will be the one to pass them on." "Good suggestion." Elland smiled. White Shark Hamilton's face darkened as he looked around. He suddenly threw out his hand, slapping the bartender in the face. The bartender flew out, all his teeth dropping to the floor. Klein quietly watched from five meters away, as if none of this had anything to do with him. Chapter 500: Interrogating White Shark Bam! The bartender fell to the ground, writhing in pain. White Shark Hamilton snorted without saying anything. He turned around and walked toward the second floor, stepping on the creaking wooden stairs. Once the excitement ended, the drunkards dispersed one by one. Unperturbed, Captain Elland and the others returned upstairs to continue playing cards. Klein took the opportunity to follow. He returned to Flying Fish & Wine not to deal with White Shark, who posed no threat to him, but simply to obtain more information from the bar's owner, who was connected to many pirate factions. After all, he had named his new identity Gehrman, which carried a secret implication: hunting bloodstained pirates. He planned to use their souls, flesh, and Beyonder characteristics to replace the souls within Creeping Hunger that awaited release. There was no gas in Damir Harbor, and the corridor on the second floor was quite dark. The brass candleholders set into the walls burned dimly, their flames flickering. Klein observed the surroundings as he wiped his face, silently transforming into one of the bouncers on the first floor. He used Illusion Creation to compensate for his mismatched attire. After finishing his preparations, he walked toward the room that his spiritual intuition identified as White Shark Hamilton's. He passed the card room without attracting attention. He stopped in front of the bouncers who were guarding the corridor and said in a low voice, "There's something going on downstairs again." "Holy Lord of Storms, what's happening tonight?" a bouncer sighed. "I hope those women don't get hurt," another guard said worriedly. He was referring to the prostitutes who conducted their business in the bar. "They're fine." Klein stepped past the bouncers and knocked on White Shark's door. "Who is it?" Hamilton asked warily. "Boss, it's me. Something happened downstairs again!" Klein recalled what he had learned while observing the earlier commotion and deliberately hoarsened his voice. "Damn it!" Hamilton bellowed. "Come in and explain to me what happened!" Klein turned the doorknob and walked in. When he closed the door, he dispelled the illusion, and the muscles on his face rapidly squirmed, returning him to his previous identity: the new customer with blond hair, blue eyes, and ordinary facial features. "You..." Hamilton was stunned for a moment before he immediately opened his mouth wide in an attempt to shout loudly. At the same time, many illusory fish scales surfaced on the back of his hand, his already large, fat body swelling. Suddenly, his heart began to beat faster, and a powerful, instinctive fear seized him by the throat. At this moment, he felt that the stranger standing by the door was a demon that had been starved for many days, regarding his flesh and soul with an icy, ravenous gaze. All of a sudden, White Shark Hamilton was seized by such extreme panic that he could not react effectively. Klein slowly walked to the sofa and sat down. He smiled politely. "Now, can we talk calmly?" The feeling of being stared at by a horrifying monster suddenly disappeared. Hamilton relaxed abruptly while his body shrank like a punctured balloon. He didn't recklessly call for help. Beads of sweat covered his forehead as he asked, "Who are you? What do you want?" "A hunter," Klein answered casually. "I heard that you're connected to a number of pirate factions. I would like to know details about each of them." "No, I'm not..." White Shark Hamilton denied reflexively. He immediately sensed that extreme hunger again, as if the man's eyes had taken on a dark red hue. Klein considered his persona inwardly and said with a gentlemanly smile, "You have two choices. "First, answer frankly. Second, let me kill you, then answer honestly." Kill me and channel my spirit? White Shark Hamilton had heard of similar rumors. He swallowed hard and asked, "Why do you want to know about any of this?" Klein smiled and answered, "I'm a hunter, so I'm hunting bounties." Hamilton suddenly felt that the man's polite smile was tinged with an indescribable madness, and he couldn't help but blurt out, "Are... Are you mad? "I've seen many similar adventurers, but all of them have been buried at the bottom of the sea! "It's not hard to kill a lone pirate, but can you defend against any further retaliation? The whores in the bar or the seemingly ordinary customers might all be pirate informants! Your friendly accomplice could be bribed at any moment and you'll be shot in the back! The pirates will gather information in advance and surround your ship. Can you protect all the passengers? Can you survive a cannon bombardment? On the sea where there's no room to run, how are you going to survive?" After venting his terror in one breath, he saw the man, who called himself a hunter, give a gentle, kindly smile. "Just kill them all, and then there won't be such a problem." A true madman... White Shark Hamilton immediately took a deep breath and said, "I'm in contact with many pirates, but it's a passive relationship. They need to dispose of the cash, jewelry, and goods they plunder in exchange for alcohol, food, fresh water, weapons, and the company of women. They have to do that through me, but I can only wait here for them. I don't know where their ships sail or where they're going." "What else?" Klein asked calmly. His reply just now was mainly to scare White Shark. As for pirate retaliation, he wasn't worried at all. As a Faceless, he might as well find a place to drown himself at the bottom of the sea if they could find him so easily. And... White Shark Hamilton's throat bobbed, but he gave neither an affirmative nor a negative response. He clamped his mouth shut and looked at the gentleman in the half top hat. The gentleman's eyes were calm and reserved, as though madness was brewing within him. The uneasy silence resembled the calm sea before a storm, with hidden currents colliding and gathering force. Finally, Hamilton shifted his gaze away and slapped a hand on the desk in frustration. "Yes, I'm still gathering information for them. If I learn anything urgent, I'll use the radio transceiver they gave me to alert them." White Shark didn't dare take the risk. He was afraid that the man possessed unique Beyonder powers that could determine whether he was speaking truthfully and telling the whole truth. "Radio transceiver?" Klein, who had won his gamble, immediately seized on the term. "That's what they called it when explaining it to me. It's like a telegram, but it doesn't require a wire." Hamilton turned and walked over to the gray safe and squatted down. A wireless telegraph? The pirates possess such advanced technology? Klein could vaguely guess what the radio transceiver was. He had previously thought of inventing something like this, but when he flipped through relevant magazines, he realized that wireless telegraphs had long since appeared. However, they had not found their place in the commercial world. The Berserk Sea, which separated the Northern and Southern Continents with its constant thunder and lightning, chaotic magnetic fields, and violent storms, made only a few sea routes accessible. Even when ships were equipped with wireless telegraphs, the devices were almost useless. Similarly, the weather in the Fog Sea and Sonia Sea changed drastically, and numerous factors affected electromagnetic transmission. The use of wireless telegraphy had been severely restricted. Could it be that there's an enhanced model that can solve some of those problems? Klein watched as White Shark pried open the floor plank in front of the safe and twisted a mechanism to reveal a secret door in the wall. Behind the secret door was a hidden cabinet with three levels. On the top level were some documents and bills, a revolver, a new type of half-arm gun, and other weapons, while the bottom layer was filled with complicated black machinery. With just a glance, Klein deduced from his previous life's impressions and the information he had gathered that the machine was a radio transceiver. "They call it a radio transceiver. Its transmissions can be received by similar devices as far away as the Rorsted Archipelago. Any farther and it'll depend on the weather and one's luck. Usually, it's cumbersome and severely limited." Hamilton didn't know much about the machine, so he described its capabilities vaguely based on his experience and instructions. It's better than the new radio transceivers that are now being commercialized... I wonder who invented it... Klein quietly listened and asked, "Who are they?" He made himself look like a bounty hunter who didn't understand technology. White Shark Hamilton wiped the cold sweat from his forehead and said, "Silver Coin Viper Oder, who claims to serve the owner of the Dawn, and Old Quinn, Admiral of Blood's intelligence officer. They appeared together, and I can't be certain whether they're working together. Of course, Oder has never offered more than claims." The owner of the Dawn, that Queen Mystic? Klein looked away, a gold coin appearing in his hand. The gold coin kept weaving between his fingers before finally leaping into the air and landing. White Shark watched in puzzled trepidation. Glancing down at it, Klein slowly stood up. At this moment, he suddenly asked, "Who gave you the potion?" "O-Old Quinn..." Hamilton hesitated but still chose to answer honestly. Klein nodded and asked nothing further. He turned and walked toward the door. Thud! The wooden door opened and closed. The figure wearing a black coat disappeared from White Shark's room. Hamilton held his breath, waited for more than ten seconds, and finally let out a long sigh. He quickly wiped the sweat from his face, placed the radio transceiver on the desk, flipped through a codebook, and hurriedly sent a telegram: "I was targeted! "By an unfamiliar fellow!" Beside the completely engrossed Hamilton, Klein had his hands in his pockets as he quietly watched, memorizing the full frequency band and the codes. His apparent departure had been nothing more than a large-scale illusion, more than enough to deal with a Low-Sequence Beyonder on the Sailor pathway like White Shark. As for whether he could recall the details later, a Seer had no need to worry. A dream divination was enough to recall everything. Admiral of Blood and his men delight in killing and bloodshed. They subject women to brutal violence. Every time they rob a passenger ship, they leave tragedy behind... This is common knowledge, and they themselves are proud of it. They never hesitate to boast about it... The target of the hunt and the risk involved--I'll give priority to them... Klein thought for a moment and then prepared to leave the room while Hamilton tidied and put away the radio transceiver. He didn't plan on dealing with White Shark for the time being, as he was afraid of disturbing the real prey. Anyway, a man like him, fixed to a territory on land, could easily be dealt with later by sending a letter exposing his crimes. Klein's soundless footsteps caused the door to slowly open and then quietly close, letting in a faint, cool breeze.
